# Every word on www.pomdr.org, by page

Fetched fresh from the live site on 2026-10-22, every page reachable from the menu, plus the 27 board and staff pages and the 78 individual dog pages linked from the lists. Words are in page order. Headings keep their level, lists keep their bullets, links show as [text](address), and [image: ...] is an image's alt text (the words a screen reader hears). The header, menu and footer are the same on every page and appear once, in section 1. The applications and donation pages hold a Little Green Light form whose questions live in LGL, so those pages show only what the site itself says around the form.

The site itself is at https://www.pomdr.org (which serves https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org). Section 2 follows the menu order and tucks each page under the menu page that links to it. Section 3 is the people pages and section 4 the dog pages. The earlier archive with raw HTML is in `docs/archive/live-site-2026-10-16/`.

Pages: 58 distinct pages (plus 27 people pages and 78 dog pages). Words on the site today, chrome excluded: about 114,011.

#### POMDR Videos

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/videos.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 1291

#### POMDR Videos
We hope you enjoy watching these videos that showcase our work.
This is Our Message to You
Every day POMDR staff and volunteers provide the tender loving care and gentle reassurance to dogs in need. Our message to the dogs and to our supporters is that “every little thing is going to be alright.” With your support we can keep that promise over and over again. This video features the song “Three Little Birds” as sung by Clay and Kelsy and by Bob Marley
Created by Monica Rua
Advisory Council/Co-Founder, 2026
Take a Chance on POMDR
Behind every dog we help and every person we help is a team of staff, volunteers, donors and adopters who make magic happen! Take a chance on POMDR so we can change the lives of more senior people and senior dogs. This video features the song “Take a Chance on Me” as sung by ABBA.
Created by Monica Rua
Advisory Council/Co-Founder, 2025
Making Good
Kirby from the Making Good TV show travels to California to volunteer with POMDR for several days. As Kirby serves people in need, he learns just how big of an impact the dogs have on their guardians' wellness.
Created by Making Good, July 2024
You Are Not Alone
POMDR is a safety net for our community so senior dogs and senior people are not alone. Meet many of the dogs and people we were able to help in 2023. This video features the song “You Will Be Found” as sung by Ben Platt.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2024
Weathering the Storm
POMDR was able to rescue Stormy, a blind Chihuahua mix, after he was caught in a storm drain. Meet many of the other dogs and people we were able to help in 2022. This video features the song “Unchained Melody” as sung by Lykke Li.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2023
CNN Heroes
POMDR is honored to have our work recognized by CNN. Watch their video that highlights stories from our Rescue, Adoption and Helping Paw Programs as well as POMDR’s origin story.
Created by CNN, 2022
Changes
POMDR has met the challenges of the many changes facing our community and the planet over the last few years. We are proud to be a nimble organization that can change with the times to best serve the most vulnerable members of our community. Meet some of the dogs, cats and people we helped in 2021. This video features the song “Landslide” by Fleetwood Mac.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2022
Grandpa Joe's Golden Ticket
Grandpa Joe came to POMDR in terrible condition. He couldn’t walk and he was vision impaired, thin, and withdrawn. Named after the character in Charlie and the Chocolate Factory, our Grandpa Joe was able to cash in his golden ticket too. With plenty of TLC, vet care, physical therapy, and by making a connection with his foster family, Grandpa Joe summoned the will to live and thrive. Watch his amazing transformation. This video features the song “(I've Got a) Golden Ticket)” as sung by Jack Alberston & Peter Ostrum.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2022
2020: A “Ruff” Year
POMDR was called to help more people and animals than ever in 2020 due to the pandemic, rampant unemployment and financial hardship throughout our community, and devastating California wildfires. With the support of our amazing supporters, we were able to step up and help those in need. Meet Sunny Delight, the last rescue of 2020 and find out how her story went from heartbreaking to heartwarming. This video features the song “Angel by the Wings” by Sia.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2021
Dapper: Don't Give Up
Meet Dapper. Dapper endured years of neglect and cruelty. He had been living in a tiny enclosure, outside, for most of his life with little human contact or care. He was finally confiscated by animal control when he was 16 years old and transferred into the care of POMDR. Dapper has endured the toughest of circumstances and persevered. He teaches us all to never give up. His story has a truly happy ending. Watch the video to witness his transformation. This video features the song “Don’t Give Up” by Peter Gabriel with Kate Bush.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2020
POMDR During COVID-19
On March 19, 2020, the state of California was ordered to shelter-in-place due to the COVID-19 pandemic. The result was POMDR became busier than we had ever been. Volunteer applications doubled, adoption applications tripled, Helping Paw applications quadrupled and we rose to the challenge. We are grateful to be able to continue to serve those in our community who are most vulnerable. This video features the song “Rise Up” as sung by Andra Day.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2020
POMDR Vet Clinic Opens!
Follow the journey of the building of the POMDR vet clinic as well as Autumn’s story. Autumn had been hit by a car and had a broken pelvis and a mangled leg. She was one of the first patients treated at the POMDR Boand Clinic. The clinic will enable POMDR to treat thousands of dogs in the years to come. This video features the song “You’re Still the One” as sung by Shania Twain.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2020
A Decade of POMDR
Watch a retrospective celebrating POMDR’s first ten years including milestones, highlights, a thank you to our kind and generous volunteers and supporters, and some special words from the dogs! This video features the song “Kind and Generous” by Natalie Merchant and “Walking on Sunshine” by Katrina and the Waves. It debuted at our Fourth Annual Lucky Dog Gala, in March 2019.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2019
Stories
Follow the stories of several dogs as they come into our program, receive TLC in a foster home, and get adopted by their forever families. Also, meet a few of the dogs who were able to stay with their guardians thanks to our Helping Paw Program. This video features the song “The Story” by Brandi Carlile. It debuted at our Third Annual Lucky Dog Gala, in March 2018.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2018
Our Mission
Hear more about why POMDR was started and what volunteers and supporters are saying about our mission. Includes special commentary by Dina Eastwood and Debra Couch as well as footage of many wonderful senior dogs! This video was produced by Trucksis Enterprises, Inc. Media Productions.
Created by TEI, Inc., 2017
Lean on Me
Meet several of the dogs and people whose lives were touched by POMDR. This video highlights many of the dog who were surrendered to POMDR and the dogs POMDR brought in from animal shelters. You will also meet several Helping Paw clients who were able to keep their pets with a little assistance from POMDR. This heartwarming video features the song “Lean on Me” by Bill Withers. It debuted at our Second Annual Lucky Dog Gala, in March 2017.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2017
Lucky Dogs
Meet Lucky and Patches, two of the Lucky Dogs who came to POMDR, as well as many other dogs who found their happy homes. This heartwarming video features the song “Fix You” by Coldplay. It was shown at our First Annual Lucky Dog Gala, in March 2016.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2016
About Us
Learn about POMDR and what we do as told by the dogs and people we’ve helped and some of the people who make it all happen. This fun video features the song “I’m a Believer” by Neil Diamond. It was first shown at our Ulti-Mutt Appetizer Party, in December 2014.
Created by Monica Rua
Board President/Co-Founder, 2014

### POMDR About Us

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/aboutus.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 660

#### About Us
Peace of Mind Dog Rescue (POMDR) is a 501(c)(3) nonprofit organization founded in October 2009. Our tax ID number (TIN) is 27-1154816.
Our mission is to be a resource and advocate for senior dogs and senior people on California’s Central Coast. We focus on helping dogs and people from Monterey, Santa Cruz, and San Benito counties.
We find loving foster and forever homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them and for senior dogs in shelters. We also provide volunteer and financial assistance so seniors can keep their pets with them as long as possible. In addition, we make pre-arrangements to take in dogs should their guardians become unable to care for them.
We have a lifetime commitment to all the dogs who come into our care. They will either be adopted into a wonderful, permanent home, or will live out their lives in one of our foster homes. (Sometimes when we bring a senior dog into our care, it becomes evident that he or she should not have to endure one more move or life change.)
We help senior citizens pay for veterinary care for their dogs if they are unable to afford medical expenses. We also provide temporary foster care for people who are hospitalized and dog walking for people who are no longer able to walk their dogs.
POMDR is led by a dedicated board and an incredible staff. POMDR is also supported by a network of over 1500 volunteers who help with foster care, adoption events, transportation, office work, fundraising, and other tasks as they come up. We pride ourselves on adhering to the [POMDR culture](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRCulture.html).
Our vision is to model lifetime care for dogs and all companion animals to help bring about a positive change in the way society thinks about and treats senior dogs, and to create better lives for them through rescue, foster, adoption, hospice and education.
POMDR has adopted a Compassionate Food Policy for our organization. We believe that all animals should be treated humanely and with compassion throughout their lives. One way POMDR chooses to support the concept of compassion to all living beings is to serve only vegetarian food at all POMDR events, from small meetings of staff and volunteers, to large gatherings such as our annual Lucky Dog Gala. We recommend the same but do not control food options when outside organizations are the primary host or sponsor of an event.
Diversity, Equity and Inclusion are important to POMDR. This includes staff, volunteers, adopters, and Helping Paw clients. We do not tolerate discrimination and harassment of any kind based on race, color, sex, religion, sexual orientation, national origin, disability, genetic information, or pregnancy. POMDR makes hiring decisions based solely on qualifications, merit, and business needs at the time. POMDR makes adoption decisions based on the best match for the dog and adopter taking into account the dog’s activity level, special needs, size, health, and temperament.
Our offices and headquarters are located at the POMDR Patricia J Bauer Center at 615 Forest Avenue in Pacific Grove.

#### POMDR Culture

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRCulture.html  
Linked from: POMDR About Us  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 149

#### POMDR Culture
Feedback: To be open to positive and constructive feedback from supervisors, co-workers, volunteer, and general public in an effort to take advantage of all opportunities to grow and improve.
Best Practices: To continually strive to explore best practices and ways to improve our programs, policies, procedures and organization effectiveness.
Positive Attitude: To maintain a solution oriented, optimistic outlook on our ability to make a difference.
Solution-driven: To strive for creative solutions to any challenges that we may face.
Responsibility: To agree that it is everyone’s responsibility to create a positive experience for volunteers, guardian surrenders, donors, adopters and the general public whether or not what they need is in our job description.
Professional: To hold ourselves to the utmost standards of professionalism in appearance and attitude at all times.
Volunteer and Donor Appreciation: To express our sincere appreciation for our volunteers and donors in every interaction.

#### POMDR Other Adoptable Dogs

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/courtesylistings.php  
Linked from: POMDR Adoptable Dogs  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 1810

[Our Adoptable Dogs](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adopt.php)
Other Adoptable Dogs
[Hospice Care](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/hospicedogs.php)
[Adopted Dogs](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adopted.php)
#### Other Adoptable Dogs - Courtesy Listings
The following dogs are not under the care of Peace of Mind Dog Rescue. We provide these listings as a courtesy to the community to help these dogs find new homes. Please contact the person named in the dog's writeup if you're interested in finding out more about one of them.
Mikki
Posted 10/16/2026
[image: Mikki]
Mikki is truly a gentleman. At just 3 years old, he has a lot of love to give, but he knows how to keep it mellow and low-key. He is polite with other dogs, great with children, and good with cats--even if they put him in his place when he gets rambunctious. He does best when introduced to new dogs mindfully and slowly, but he can settle with them well!
Mikki's former guardian developed dementia and could no longer care for him. After his guardian's children stepped up, they lost their home to a fire. Despite everything, Mikki has persevered and shown that even through hard times, one can have a kind heart. All he asks for is a safe place to call his own and a family to love unconditionally.
To learn more about Mikki, contact Pia at [email protected].
Gertrude
Posted 10/14/2026
[image: Gertrude]
Meet Gertrude! The most happy, wiggly Boxer mix you are bound to meet! This sweet senior lady is a wee bit underweight at only 37lbs but we expect will stay on the smaller side. She's estimated to be around 10 years young. She gets along well with other dogs and of course loves her people.
Gertrude is currently at the Los Banos Animal Shelter. This is a small county animal control where space is limited. Gertrude is in need of a bit of luck to find her forever home. If you are interested in Gertrude, please call or drop by the Los Banos Animal Shelter today at (209) 827-7089.
Chimmu
Updated 9/10/2026
[image: Chimmu]
This incredibly gorgeous boy is none other than Chimmu! He's an Indian Pariah / Retriever/ Whippet Mix. who is about 4 years and around 40lbs. This special guy was rescued along with his momma from the streets of India by their loving foster but with many dogs of her own, she is looking for that perfect landing spot for these great pups. Because of his rough past he is a bit scared when new people come to the house or when seeing unknown dogs out in the world.
If you'd like to learn more about Chimmu please contact Morella at 415-858-9731 or by email at Morellab@aol.com.
Chikki
Updated 9/10/2026
[image: Chikki]
"I'm tired of living with 5 other dogs that we don't get along. Please help my foster Mom she loves me so much but she needs to find me a loving quiet home without other doggies nor cats." - Chikki
Chikki is 6 years old and she is better sweet. She was rescued from the streets of India where she gave birth to 4 puppies who were all adopted except Chimmu. You can see her scars on her face from fighting with monkey. to survive and keep her puppies safe. She would thrive in a quiet home with no other pets nor small children
If you'd like to learn more about Chikki please contact Morella at 415-858-9731 or by email at Morellab@aol.com.
Gypsy
Updated 9/8/2026
[image: Gypsy]
Gypsy is a Shepherd mix looking for a new home after her guardian sadly passed away. This lovely girl is estimated to be four years old and approximately 50lbs. She is comfortable alone and with dogs. She happened to find her previous guardian by wandering onto their property, and she's now ready for her next adventure--wandering into your heart! Gypsy is house and crate trained and knows all her basic commands! She is spayed and has all her vaccinations completed.
If you'd like to learn more about Gypsy, reach out to Annette at (408) 921-1534.
Bailey Bae
Updated 9/8/2026
[image: Bailey Bae]
Bailey is a beautiful, affectionate gal with the most loving big brown eyes, a smile that wins people over instantly, and with her playful energy, most people are shocked to hear she's 9. She loves daily walks, cuddle time, and being close to her person. She is 30 lbs. and working toward her ideal weight of around 24 lbs., where she'll truly be feeling her best, and naturally long-haired but groomed every few months.
You can find her on Instagram at @its_baileybae.
She is very smart and familiar with her basic commands, including sit, stay, come, leave it, shake (left and right!), and spin. Bailey loves 2 walks a day, but is happy with one solid outing -- whether that's a neighborhood walk, a hike (she can comfortably cover several miles), or a trip to the beach (she's not a water girl, which conveniently keeps her clean!). Bonus points if she spots a lizard to chase or gopher holes to investigate. And don't let her age fool you -- get her going with a squeaky toy and she plays like a puppy.
Bailey requires a child-free home where she can feel safe, relaxed, and fully herself. She is fearful and reactive around young children, so a home without them is a must. She also truly shines as the only pet -- no competing for attention, just all the love directed at her. She would thrive with someone (or a couple) who can give her plenty of company, whether that's a retiree, someone who works from home, or a person who can bring her along to work.
If you'd like to learn more about Bailey, please contact Michelle at (408) 250-4422
Luna
Posted 8/29/2026
[image: Luna]
Luna a 9-year-old rat terrier ready to seek out her next adventure. She is 19lbs of personality! When meeting new people, she likes to take her time, but soon after, her friendly personality shines through! That being said, she is not very fond of children and would prefer an adult-only household. She also loves to chase cats and would do best without cats.
She is fine with other dogs, though she prefers to have her space and do her own thing--a truly independent lady! And like a true lady, she is house-trained, crate-trained, walks well on leash, and knows her basic commands. Best of all, she has no separation anxiety.
If you are interested in learning more about Luna, please contact Tiffanie at: (707) 888-7406
Wilson and Walter
Posted 8/12/2026
[image: Wilson and Walter]
What's better than one pup? Two! Wilson and Walter are a bonded pair of senior boys looking for a home where they can spend their golden years together. Wilson is a 13-year-old Dachshund mix, and Walter is a 11-year-old Yorkie/Jack Russell Terrier mix - between the two of them, they've got a whole lot of love and a whole lot of personality. They are crate trained, walk well on leash, and do well with people and other dogs. Wilson is the laid-back one who's happy to follow your lead, while Walter brings a little extra spunk to the duo. These two have been together their whole lives, and they're hoping to find someone who will keep it that way.
For more information about Wilson and Walter, please contact Teresa at (831) 706-1576 or Kelly at (828) 337-1765.
Appa
Updated 7/17/2026
[image: Appa]
Appa is a friendly, quiet, and smart 90 pound German Shepard, husky mix. He is 5 year old love bug who gets along great with dogs, cats and even bunnies! He is calm and patient with children and understands the "relax" command. He is house trained, crate trained and knows all his basic commands well (sit, stay, down etc.) and walks well on leash. He does well when left alone but would love to accompany you on your weekend hikes, daily runs or coffee dates!
If Appa sounds like the perfect new addition to your family and you would like more information, please contact Ana at Anarosales169@gmail.com.
Obi
Updated 7/17/2026
[image: Obi]
Sweet Obi is a 7-year-old terrier mix, about 14 lbs. Obi is mostly a homebody. Mellow and quiet, he loves cozying up in blankets all day and enjoys snuggles and cuddles. Once he forms a connection, he becomes very lovey-dovey. Short walks or hikes in quiet areas are his favorite, especially when he gets to sniff everything at his own pace. He doesn't need much exercise and isn't a high-energy dog, so working from home with him close by is easy and distraction-free.
Food is a huge motivator for him. He's the kind of dog who won't refuse a meal, even when he's not feeling well. Kongs and rodent-shaped stuffed animals are his favorite toys. He knows all the main commands (sit, jump, come, wait, stay, relax, crate, etc.) and is fully house-trained. Although he has separation anxiety and can be protective of his parents, he's also been incredibly brave and smart, making real progress in facing his fears. He deserves the chance to keep healing and building confidence.
If you are interested in learning more about Obi, please contact Andrea at 669-240-6425 or by email at [email protected]
Kimber
Posted 7/9/2026
[image: Kimber]
Kimber is a Husky looking for a new home after her family has to relocate and can no longer give her the space and attention she deserves. This beautiful girl has been loved since the day she was born - and it shows! She is 8 years old, approximately 75lbs, and has been with her family her whole life.
Kimber is the full Husky package: she can be perfectly mellow and cuddly one moment, and a total ball of energy the next. She loves people of all kinds, including children, and has never met a dog she didn't like. True to her breed, she's got plenty to say on walks, especially when she spots a new furry friend to get excited about!
She is house trained, walks well on leash, and knows her basic commands. Kimber does best with access to outdoor space where she can run and burn off that Husky energy - so a home with a yard would be ideal. Her family describes her as a loving, caring sweetheart, and they are heartbroken to let her go. They just want to make sure she lands somewhere that can truly give her the life she deserves.
If you are interested in learning more about Kimber, please reach out to Kevin at (831) 277-8458
Ms. Daisy
Posted 7/7/2026
[image: Ms. Daisy]
Ms. Daisy is a proper lady. At 11 years old, she is wisened but still loves walks and plays with her toys. She's 52lbs of love! She adores people - adults and kids alike. Though she can be protective at times, she is overall a very relaxed girl.
She has good etiquette; she is house trained, walks well on leash, and knows her basic obedience. She is also fine being left alone, snoozing through her "me time." She doesn't warm up to other dogs well, but isn't known to instigate either.
Ms. Daisy is described as a great family dog. If you'd like to learn more about Ms. Daisy, please contact Shannon at (408) 807-0007.

#### POMDR Hospice Dogs

Linked from: POMDR Other Adoptable Dogs

Words: 927

[Our Adoptable Dogs](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adopt.php)
[Other Adoptable Dogs](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/courtesylistings.php)
Hospice Care
[Adopted Dogs](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adopted.php)
#### Hospice Care
Sometimes when we bring a senior dog into our care, we find out they have a life threatening medical condition. For some of our dogs who are expected to live only a few months, we provide Hospice Care.
Providing Hospice Care for a dog is a big commitment and a rewarding experience. If you are interested in providing foster care for a dog for the last several months or more of their life, [contact us](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#aac3c4ccc5eadacfcbc9cfc5ccc7c3c4cecec5cdd8cfd9c9dfcf84c5d8cd). As with all foster assignments, POMDR will cover all medical expenses for dogs in hospice care.
Please consider becoming a sponsor for one of these sweeties.
Huey
Sponsored By: Barbara Frates, Roland D. Zoller
[Sponsor Huey](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Huey)
Huey is an old soul who has mastered the art of being a very goodest of boys. Sweet Huey joined us from the Animal Resource Center of the Inland Empire after he was found as a stray. Due to their over full shelter, they felt he would to best in the hands of a rescue that could help ensure he landed in the right adoptive home.
Sadly, shortly after his arrival with us, Huey's health began to decline. After a few emergency vet visits and testing, his medical team suspect an intestinal cancer is the reason for the decline. We're so grateful that Huey has the support of our vet team and his loving foster home to meet his needs in this challenging time where he will remain for the rest of his days.
Please consider [sponsoring Huey's forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Huey)
Gigi (aka Spork)
[Sponsor Gigi (aka Spork)](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Gigi (aka Spork))
Sweet little miss Spork- or Gigi as her hospice foster calls her, joined us from San Jose Animal Care Center and Services after she was found wandering the streets as a stray, due to her severe medical needs they thought she would shine best in a senior-focused rescue and they were not wrong. She quickly became a staff favorite upon arrival and has found a loving landing spot in her hospice-foster's home.
While in our program, we found Spork to need liver support, have Cushing's disease, kidney disease, and to have a heart murmur. Due to the medical support that she needs daily, we found it best to make her a permanent POMDR pup to reside in her loving foster home.
Please consider [sponsoring Gigi (aka Spork)'s forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Gigi (aka Spork))
Winkle
Sponsored By: Boo Boo Foundation
[Sponsor Winkle](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Winkle)
Sweet Winkle came to POMDR from another rescue group when they discovered that his medical needs were more than they could handle. Senior dogs like Winkle with senior-related issues are our specialty so we were happy to bring him into our program. As time has gone by, Winkle developed cataracts and has lost most of his vision. It seems his senior years have caught up to him as well as he has been experiencing some doggie dementia.
Luckily Winkle hit the jackpot with a loving foster mom able to tend to his needs and wonderful furry foster siblings to support him. Winkle will live out his time with all of the love and support he could ask for.
Please consider [sponsoring Winkle's forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Winkle)
Snowflake
Sponsored By: Kira Steinberg, Wente Tran, Elbina Rafizadeh, Kathy Kirros, BooBoo Foundation
[Sponsor Snowflake](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Snowflake)
Sweet little Snowflake is every bit as unique and dainty as her name suggests. This special senior lady came to Peace of Mind after being picked up as a stray by Hitchcock Road Animal Services in Salinas.
Once in our care, Snowflake was found to have some significant medical needs, including arthritis, multiple heart conditions, and ongoing eye care. Thankfully, this little lady hit the jackpot when she found a loving foster family who welcomed her with open arms and were happy to give her the comfort and care she deserved in her golden years. Given her medical needs, they knew she deserved a place where she could stay comfortable, cherished, and well cared for for the rest of her life. So, along with her beloved guinea pig sibling, Milkshake, Snowflake is officially home for good.
Please consider [sponsoring Snowflake's forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Snowflake)
Zelda
Sponsored By: Joan Best
[Sponsor Zelda](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Zelda)
Sweet Zelda came to POMDR from Santa Clara County Animal Services after her guardian sadly was hospitalized and unable to care for her.
In her time with POMDR, Zelda has proven herself to be tiny but mighty. She loves being carried by her foster mom and is always content to toddle around the yard in the sun.
Zelda came to us with one of her eyes needing to be removed and a heart murmur. Sadly in time she has declined and her heart murmur has worsened, her arthritis has become more prominent and she has developed the early stages of kidney disease. Due to her medical needs, her loving foster has decided to be her forever landing spot to continue to support her senior needs.
Please consider [sponsoring Zelda's forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Zelda)
Suki
Sponsored By: Cathleen and Marty Cohen
[Sponsor Suki](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Suki)
Suki's name comes from the Japanese word for loved one, and it fits her perfectly. This sweet girl has a gentle, mellow soul and a calm presence that makes you want to sit beside her and tell her everything will be okay.
Sweet Suki joins us from Hitchcock Road Animal Services after she was found as a stray. Upon arrival it was clear that she needed some additional TLC and a special landing spot. We're so grateful that she found her hospice-foster extraordinaire to meet her higher medical needs.
Please consider [sponsoring Suki's forever care.](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRSponsorDog.php?petname=Suki)

#### POMDR Adoption Process

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adoptionprocess.html  
Linked from: POMDR Adoptable Dogs  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 283

#### Our Adoption Process
Thank you for your interest in adopting a dog!
Each dog in our program receives a thorough veterinary exam upon intake. The senior dogs also get a senior blood panel and in many cases a dental cleaning and any necessary extractions. Each dog is altered, microchipped, and vaccinated unless our veterinarian advises against sterilization surgery or vaccines due to age or other health concerns.
Adoption fees range from $155-$305 depending upon the age and health of the dog and expected future medical expenses that will be taken on by the adopter.
Our dogs are kept in loving foster homes. The first step to meeting an adoptable dog is to fill out an online adoption questionnaire. If you are a good match, we will connect you with the dog’s foster to talk more, then set up a meet and greet with the dog.
Responses to our adoption questionnaires are typically sent via email - please check your spam/junk file to ensure you are getting the latest updates on your adoption.
All adopters must agree to an initial home check and annual follow up calls throughout the life of the dog in order to adopt one of the dogs entrusted to our care. We do appreciate your willingness to open your heart and your home to a dog in need of a permanent family. We take our role of placing dogs in a new home very seriously and do everything we can to ensure the dogs we place are well cared for throughout their lives.
[Wondering whether to adopt a senior dog?](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/whyadoptseniordog.html)
Whether you adopt a senior dog or a younger dog, we thank you for helping to save a life!

#### POMDR Why Adopt a Senior Dog?

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/whyadoptseniordog.html  
Linked from: POMDR Adoption Process  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 809

#### Why Should I Adopt a Senior Dog?
There are so many great reasons to adopt an older dog... Senior dogs have outgrown puppy behaviors like destructive chewing and are often already house-trained. Many have been loved dogs and just want their second chance at being part of a family. Others may have been neglected and you have the opportunity to make them feel truly loved for the first time in their life!
A senior dog’s gratitude, love and loyalty is like no other. Your senior dog will thank you forever for seeing past their grey muzzle to the love they still have to give!
#### Every Dog Deserves a Second Chance
Senior pets are at a much higher risk of being euthanized at a shelter. By bringing a senior dog into your family, you are truly saving a life. The gratitude you will see in your dog’s eyes is priceless and you’ll always know that YOU were the person who gave them a second chance at happiness.
#### They Are Calmer, Quieter and Less Destructive
Senior dogs have generally outgrown puppy behaviors like chewing and are all-around calmer dogs. So you can spare your shoes and furniture, and still welcome a wonderful new addition to the family!
#### What You See Is What You Get!
With a senior dog, you know exactly what you’re getting. Their size, breed and temperament are pretty much solidified so there are no surprises when the cute little puppy you adopt turns into a ninety-pounder!
#### They Usually Have Basic Training!
Senior dogs typically require less training. They are often already house-trained and know basic commands, taught by their previous guardians who have passed away or who can no longer care for them.
#### They Are NOT “Problem Dogs”
The vast majority of our senior dogs come to POMDR because their guardians have passed away, need to move into assisted living or simply because they can no longer care for their dog. Contrary to common misconception, these dogs haven’t done anything wrong to end up homeless. Most senior pups are house-trained, loved their previous families and were loyal companions for their years prior to ending up at the shelter.
#### Every Day is Special
A common concern expressed is that an older dog won’t be in your life as long as a puppy would. While this may be true, their gratitude, love and loyalty is just as true. If anything, they’ll teach you to value every day you have the honor of spending with them that much more and they’ll thank you forever for seeing past their grey muzzles to the love they still have to give.
#### Testimonials
The true testimonials about what it is like to adopt a senior rescue are best spoken by those who have opened their home to a wonderful, older pet:
“Getting to watch an animal go from neglected and…just plain sad to enjoying his new life is just incredibly rewarding in a way that’s very different from raising a puppy and molding him in your own image.” –Wes, IndefinitelyWild.Gizmodo.com
“They appreciate the smallest gestures--cuddles, a nap on the bed, a brisk walk--because they had to ‘go without’ at some point. It makes them, and more importantly us humans, appreciate every little thing, a bit more!” –Dina Eastwood, POMDR Adopter & Volunteer
“Sasha was the first senior dog that I adopted. Though I had fostered many dogs before her, Sasha chose me. While I thought I might get 4-5 years with her, she is still healthy and happy over 5 years later. Having a senior dog makes me cherish the time that I have with my pets even more, knowing that our time together is limited.” –Christine D., POMDR Adopter & Volunteer
“Senior dogs have more ‘life experiences under their collar,’ and because of that they are more appreciative of having a loving forever home. I have adopted many, and their wisdom and love has enhanced my life in many ways.” –Linda L., POMDR Adopter & Volunteer
“Senior dogs are usually easier to bring in to your home. They are potty trained and [it’s] so nice to have another old lady to keep me company.” –Sue C., POMDR Foster Volunteer
“I adopted Disney aka Joe and all I can say is he is the best thing that has happened to me in a long time. Everything you said about older dogs is true: they are pretty much trained when you get them and he is been such a wonderful addition to my life and even though I've had him a little over two months he has become my best little buddy and we are so bonded you would not believe it. So I want everybody out there to know this and to give these senior dogs a home. I will be forever grateful!” –Sharon S., POMDR Adopter

#### Donate to Peace of Mind Dog Rescue
We appreciate your donation of any size. POMDR relies heavily on donations from the public to further our mission. Our biggest expense is medical care for the dogs we rescue.
Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is a non-profit 501(c)(3) organization, Tax ID #27-1154816. Your donations are tax-deductible to the extent allowable by law.
Donate Now:
$60 $125 $275 $600 $1250 Other
Or choose one of these other ways to give:
[Max's Helping Paws Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?fund=Max's%20Helping%20Paws%20Fund)
To provide financial assistance to low income pet guardians needing unexpected, urgent or emergent veterinary care including surgery and specialist visits. [More info](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/maxsfund.html).
[Benefit Shop](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/benefitshop.html)
Visit the POMDR Benefit Shop at 223 Grand Ave, Suite 1 in Pacific Grove. It's where hidden gems are found. Shop and support senior dogs and senior people.
[Monthly Donation](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRMonthlyDonation.php)
Become a sustaining donor and pledge a recurring monthly donation. Your credit card will be charged your pledged amount at the first of every month.
[Tribute Donation](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?donationtype=tribute)
Make a donation in honor of or in memory of a special person or animal. We will send a special [tribute card](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/tributecard.html) announcing your gift.
[Sponsor a Dog](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/adopt.php)
Sponsor one of our adoptable dogs and help provide care. Go to our adoptable dog page, choose a dog, and click on the "Sponsor" button.
[Helping Paw](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?fund=Helping%20Paw%20Fund)
Make a donation to our [Food Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?fund=Food%20Fund) or [Helping Paw Veterinary Assistance Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?fund=Helping%20Paw%20Fund) to keep pet guardians and their pets together.
[Monica Rua Silver Hearts Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?fund=Silver%20Hearts%20Fund)
For dogs with dire medical needs including special surgeries, specialist visits, physical rehabilitation, supplements and medications. Named in honor of co-founder Monica Rua. [More info](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/silverheartsfund.html).
[Sponsor an Ad](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/sponsorad.html)
Placing ads in local newspapers helps us find homes for more dogs left behind. These ads are very successful in placing our dogs. Ad sponsorships are $120 per ad.
[Dog Tag Donor](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/dogTagDonorOp.html)
Become a Dog Tag Donor at the Boand Clinic. Your sponsorship can be in memory of or in honor of a person or animal.

Sponsor a doggie suite at the Boand Clinic for a year. Your sponsorship can be in memory of or in honor of a person or animal.

Sponsor a plaque on one of our rooms, the welcome wall, our mural, or on a garden river stone. Your sponsorship can be in memory of or in honor of a person or animal.

#### POMDR Max's Helping Paws Fund

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/maxsfund.html  
Linked from: POMDR Ways to Donate

Words: 291

#### Max’s Helping Paws Fund: A Legacy of Love, A Future of Hope
Max’s Helping Paws began with a dog named Maximillian Amadeus – Max for short – who crossed the rainbow bridge in 2016. In their grief, Max’s devoted parents, Dyana Klein and Dr. Jonathan Fradkin, chose to transform their heartbreak into hope for others. They founded Max’s Helping Paws Foundation (MHP) to honor Max’s memory by ensuring that pets in medical crisis could get the urgent veterinary care they needed, regardless of their families' financial situation.
Between 2016 and 2025, MHP helped over 2,400 pets receive life-saving treatment. Now, in a powerful new chapter, Peace of Mind Dog Rescue (POMDR) has officially adopted MHP’s life-saving programs, continuing Max’s mission through the newly established Max’s Helping Paws Fund.
Through this fund, the programs that made MHP a beacon of hope – Get 'Em to the Vet, Short-Term, and Continued Care – will not only continue, but expand. POMDR will integrate these services to help pets facing serious health crises, offering support for diagnostics, emergency procedures, surgeries, and even long-term chronic care.
Perhaps most importantly, the financial cap for veterinary assistance has increased fivefold, from $500 to up to $2,500 per case, thanks to the Max’s Helping Paws Fund. This means more pets can be treated, more lives can be saved, and more families can be spared the heartbreak of losing a beloved companion due to financial hardship.
Every donation to the Max’s Helping Paws Fund directly supports major medical care for pets in need. With your help, Max’s legacy will live on in the thousands of lives saved, one paw at a time.
[Donate to Max’s Helping Paws Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?notes=maxs_helping_paws_fund)
Founders of Max’s Helping Paws Foundation - Dyana Klein and Dr. Jonathan Fradkin

#### The Monica Rua Silver Hearts Fund
Monica with her three dogs and Shelby (a very shy Miniature Australian Shepherd foster) and Custard (a little old arthritic Chihuahua foster)
In 2024, our co-founder Monica Rua left the Board of Directors after 15 years of service to POMDR and over 27 years of serving in a leadership role in animal rescue. She is still very active with POMDR, including serving on the Advisory Board.
Monica's countless contributions over the years are invaluable. One of the ways she most loves to make a difference is by fostering and rehabilitating dogs with extreme medical and behavioral challenges.
Monica and her husband John have welcomed over 130 foster dogs into their home through the years, and they plan to continue fostering as long as they can.
Monica always gravitates to the dogs with the greatest need for care, such as dogs with severe medical conditions, fearful/shut-down dogs, super seniors (over 17 years old), dogs with mobility issues, and hospice dogs.
Meet a few of the dogs Monica has helped rehabilitate and get adopted over the years.
Grandpa Joe was found stray, nearly dead and unable to walk. He was adopted!
Beau was depressed and would barely move. He was then diagnosed with neurological problems and Cushing's. He was adopted!
Winston couldn't walk far due to neurological problems, so he would often travel by backpack or stroller. He was adopted!
Xena had terminal cancer. She spent her final weeks with love and peace.
Quincy was found as an emaciated stray. We discovered he was diabetic. He was adopted!
To honor Monica’s compassion and dedication to senior dogs and to POMDR’s mission, we started the Monica Rua Silver Hearts Fund.
This fund is used to help cover above and beyond medical expenses for the dogs POMDR takes in including special surgeries, medication, therapies, supplements, and specialist visits.
You can honor Monica’s legacy and devotion to senior dogs and senior people by donating to the Monica Rua Silver Hearts Fund.
[Donate to Silver Hearts Fund](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRDonation.php?notes=silverheartsfund)

#### Sponsor an Ad
If you or your business would like to sponsor an ad in the Carmel Pine Cone, Monterey County Weekly, the Monterey County Herald, or the Santa Cruz Good Times Weekly, please [email us](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#20494e464f6050454143454f464d494e44444f475245534355450e4f5247). We welcome sponsors who would like to sponsor a one-time ad or are interested in sponsoring one ad per month ongoing. The cost of each ad is $150.
We also welcome sponsors for radio spots on NPR stations KAZU and KUSP. NPR sponsorships cost a minimum or $1000 and are a great way to spread the word about our work.

#### Legacy Donors
Legacy Donors are an invaluable part of Peace of Mind Dog Rescue's lifesaving work for dogs left behind.
Legacy donors help strengthen the foundation of Peace of Mind Dog Rescue by helping to ensure that we will be here for our community for many years to come. Through annual donations of $5,000 or more, Legacy Donors provide critical resources necessary to support our mission of being an advocate and resource for senior dogs and senior people on California's Central Coast.
These funds provide medical care for the dogs we rescue as well as support our general operations necessary to carry out our mission.
As a Legacy Donor, you’ll be part of a compassionate community of people who love old dogs and care about the peace of mind of senior people in our community. If you would like to become a legacy donor, contact our Executive Director at (831) 718-9122 or email [info@peaceofminddogrescue.org](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#dcb5b2bab39cacb9bdbfb9b3bab1b5b2b8b8b3bbaeb9afbfa9b9f2b3aebb). She can talk with you more about our mission, our accomplishments, our goals, and why we need your support. Won't you consider becoming a Legacy Donor today?
Membership Benefits
- Direct access by phone or email to co-founders - POMDR Executive Director and President
- Invitation to visit the POMDR Bauer Center and POMDR Boand Clinic for a tour and to learn more about POMDR
- Invitation to our annual Directors' Reception and Donor Update

#### Planned Giving
Planned giving can provide you with tax benefits while simultaneously helping animals. Through a planned gift, you may be able to increase your current income or provide additional retirement income while reducing your income tax and estate taxes.
For more information, contact our Executive Director at (831) 718-9122 or email [info@peaceofminddogrescue.org](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#244d4a424b6454414547414b42494d4a40404b435641574751410a4b5643).
#### Leave a Bequest to POMDR
Please consider including POMDR in your will to leave a lasting legacy for dogs left behind. When including a bequest provision in your will, always use the full legal name and provide the federal taxpayer identification number.
Our full, legal name is Peace of Mind Dog Rescue.
Our mailing address is P.O. Box 51554, Pacific Grove, CA 93950.
Our federal taxpayer identification number is 27-1154816.
When you notify Peace of Mind Dog Rescue about your gift, whether intended or received, we will enroll you in our POMDR Legacy Circle. Legacy Circle Benefits include:
- Direct access by phone or email to co-founders - POMDR Executive Director and President
- Annual insider updates from the Executive Director
- Invitation to visit the POMDR Bauer Center for a tour and to learn more about POMDR
- Invitation to our annual Directors' reception

#### Wish List
We have an average of 60 senior dogs in our care at any given time. Each foster dog needs a set of supplies to go with them to their foster home. We are always in need of the following items to help our foster homes care for the dogs:
- Small and Medium Crates (We have an abundance of large crates.)
- Collars (No buckle collars, choke chains or pinch collars please.)
- 4 or 6 foot Leashes (No flexi leashes)
- Harnesses
- Dog Beds
- Small Dog Blankets (No large blankets or comforters)
- Bowls
- Ex Pens
- Dog Gates
- Baby Gates
- Help Em Up Harnesses
- Citronella Bark Collars and Refills
- Coats
- Sweaters
- Flea Prevention
- Gift Cards from these stores: Pet Food Express, The Raw Connection, Diggidy Dog, Stone's Pet Shop, PetSmart, Petco, Earthwise, Wishbone Pet Co., Costco, and Target
- Check out our Amazon Wish List
If you'd like to donate any items from this wish list, please bring them to the POMDR Bauer Center Monday through Saturday, 10am - 4pm. If the door is locked, ring the door bell.
Peace of Mind Dog Rescue Bauer Center
615 Forest Avenue
Pacific Grove, CA 93950
If you have any questions or would like to provide funds to purchase items from the wish list, [email us](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#c4adaaa2ab84b4a1a5a7a1aba2a9adaaa0a0aba3b6a1b7a7b1a1eaabb6a3) or call (831) 718-9122
Please consider making donations for animals. They so appreciate your generosity!

### POMDR Volunteer

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/volunteer.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 846

#### Volunteer
[image: Volunteers 2026]
Peace of Mind Dog Rescue relies on the time and talents of over 1800 volunteers to save and improve the lives of as many dogs as we can.
If you are interested in volunteering, please fill out a volunteer application.
[Fill Out Application](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRVolunteerApplication.php)
We have the following volunteer opportunities:
Foster Home – Foster volunteers are the heart and soul of what we do. We could not save the lives of dogs in need without our foster homes. Peace of Mind covers any approved medical expenses for the dogs in our care. Sometimes we may have donated food to provide our foster homes with, but, at times, you may also be required to provide high quality food for your foster dog at your expense. We can also supply donated crates, beds, blankets, toys, chewies, and treats as available. You will receive a foster manual, training, an emergency contact number, and ongoing support and behavior troubleshooting from a foster mentor assigned to work directly with you. Providing a temporary home for a dog in need can be a very rewarding experience. Your foster dog will be forever grateful. [Click here](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/fostering.html) for more information about fostering. [Click here](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/fosterneeds.html) for a complete list of dogs that need fostering.
Transportation- We often need help picking up dogs from the shelter that are ready to go to their new foster home or taking dogs to the vet for exams and surgery. If you have time during the day on week-days for this type of volunteer work, we can use your help!
Adoption Events – We need volunteers to put on adoption events once or twice a month at a variety of dog friendly locations. Adoption Event volunteers will be trained as adoption counselors and will also answer general questions from the public about our organization. If you enjoy interacting with people and would love to be part of the process of facilitating an adoption for a dog in need of a family, working at adoption events might be the perfect volunteer position for you.
Behavior and Enrichment Team – We need volunteers to work two hour shifts with a pack of dogs at our office in Pacific Grove. You will supervise group interactions as well as provide socialization, exercise, and enrichment.
Benefit Shop – Located at 223 Grand Ave. in Pacific Grove, the Benefit Shop needs volunteers to help with a variety of tasks - greeting visitors, assisting at the register, sorting donations, pricing donations, creating displays. We also need help with donation transportation. Do you have a truck to help pick up donated items as well as move unneeded items to other donation centers. We'd love your help!
Fundraising – We need volunteers willing to help out at fundraising events or to help coordinate a fundraising event. Fundraising is a very important area of volunteer work that can help us save the lives of many, many more dogs. If planning or implementing fundraising ideas is one of your interests or strengths, please let us know. We would love to hear from you.
Tabling – We need volunteers to setup an info table in front of retail venues and tell people about our organization. This is a great way for Peace of Mind to gain volunteers and supporters. If you enjoy interacting with people and getting people excited about our organization, this is the job for you!
Helping Paw - We need volunteers to help walk dogs for seniors or ill people who can no longer walk their own dogs. Sometimes we also help with transportation to the vet or groomers and other miscellaneous tasks.
Other Specialties - We often have opportunities for volunteers with specific skills, including writing, grant writing, graphic design, PR or marketing, photography, and fundraising.
General - If you want to help out, but can't commit to a specific task or specific hours, please join our volunteer brigade. You will receive periodic email requests for help with special events, helping to network with potential adopters for a specific dog, or special transportation requests. Even if you can only take on an assignment once a year, your assistance can be invaluable in helping us accomplish our mission.
Youth Volunteers (Ages 10-17) - We welcome volunteers under the age of 18 accompanied by a parent or guardian. The parent/guardian will complete the volunteer application.
- Come to an adoption event or help with dog walking accompanied by an adult.
- Do a fundraiser at your school for POMDR: a bake sale or a dog-treat sale.
- Use your social media skills to promote an adoptable dog!
- Fostering is for the entire family. As long as the adults in the home are on board, fostering can be a family project.
- Bring Peace of Mind Dog Rescue to your school! Email info@peaceofminddogrescue.org for more information.
Thanks for your interest in volunteering!
[Fill Out Application](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRVolunteerApplication.php)
