#### POMDR Fostering

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/fostering.html  
Linked from: POMDR Volunteer  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 1482

POMDR Fostering
#### Fostering - A Very Rewarding Job
We do not have a facility where our dogs are kept. All of our dogs live in foster homes as members of the family until they find their permanent home. Foster parents serve as a bridge for our dogs to transition from their previous family experience to their new homes. For dogs who have lost their family, this can be a very difficult and stressful time and they will need compassion and patience while they adjust to a new home and a new routine. For dogs that come from a shelter or from a neglectful or abusive situation, the adjustment to a loving foster home may be a dream come true for them.
Fostering can be one of the most rewarding experiences a dog lover can have. Often the foster parents feel they have received as much, if not more, than they have given. If you would like to become a foster volunteer, please fill out an [online volunteer application](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRVolunteerApplication.php).
For a complete list of dogs that need fostering [click here](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/fosterneeds.html).
Meet some of our foster parents.
Christine with Romeo
It can feel overwhelming to think of senior dogs in shelters. I can make a difference for dogs in need every time I take in a new foster dog. It is very satisfying when my foster dog finds a new family who falls in love with him or her. Then once they are adopted, I can help another dog in need find his way to a new home.
Darla with Tori
Whenever I think about the dogs my husband and I have fostered and hear or see how they are doing in their forever homes, it warms my heart, and I get a little tear of happiness. Some of these dogs have been injured, frightened strays, or their guardians could no longer take care of them. Fostering can sometimes be a challenge, but it is an incredibly satisfying experience. No matter how challenging some may be at first, all dogs seem to come fully loaded with unconditional love and affection. By the time our foster dogs found their forever homes, it was always much more difficult than we expected to let them go. So much so in fact that our last two fosters were adopted by us and we could not be happier with that decision.
Eileen with Mimi
I love fostering because I love dogs and can't adopt them all. Fostering helps everyone involved with dogs in transition. The dogs get a loving home environment, as opposed to the shelters. I get to love and nurture another dog. The new owners will hopefully have a more calm, less fearful dog to begin their journey with!
Gina with Cara
I find it difficult to not foster because I know the need is so great. We have space in our home, in our pack, and in our hearts so why not? How many opportunities do you get to improve the life and well-being of another living creature to this degree? The dogs really are so grateful. And you meet some of the nicest people, too!
Jacki with Jasmine
Fostering for POMDR I feel like a ‘matchmaker’. It is fun and interesting to meet, learn and care for dogs of various breeds, and it is super rewarding when your foster dog finds his or her special person and thrives in that forever home!
Lynn with Deja
I've had the same refrigerator magnet on various fridges for 10+years. The quote on it says "A house without a dog is not a home." I started fostering for POMDR because I needed dog love in my life. It always feels so good to bring a new foster home for the first time. To watch them start to heal from whatever trauma they just went through before they came to my home. I would give them plenty of time to adjust. The best was when they would jump up on the couch for the first time and settled down with me. Simple comforts of life.
Lynn with Gracie
Tony and I foster for selfish reasons. We enjoy the feeling of contributing to a need in our community. We delight in the behavior of dogs playing alone or with our other dogs and ourselves. We feel valued when we return to all
the happy faces and wagging tails attached to individuals who think far more of us than we deserve. We have a sense of achievement when we help transform a sick, timid and/or forgotten old dog into a healthy, confident dog with a
forever home with people who adore him or her. And our home is opened to receive another senior dog in need. These and many more are the reasons we foster. We encourage anyone who enjoys dogs to foster; the rewards are endless.
Marilee with Daisy
Animals, particularly dogs, have always been important to me and have brought tremendous amounts of love and joy into my life. I foster so that I can repay the favor and give back to dogs who are at risk of euthanasia. Dogs are amazing beings: their courage, ability to forgive and their capacity to give endless love seem to be an intrinsic part of their DNA. My foster dogs become part of the family, until wonderful people come along, tell the dogs how special and beautiful they are and then open up their homes to them with kindness and love. Every dog deserves a loving home; no dog should ever die alone, afraid and unwanted.
Polly with Maurice
Fostering is good for your soul as well as your heart. I believe this is true for the foster pet as well. I could not imagine a life without fostering. It is rewarding in more ways than I can name, and the love and thankful look you receive from the dog you are fostering is only the beginning. A precious life is put in your care, and I, as well as my family, have been repeatedly blessed with seeing how thankful these dogs are to be loved once again. We have seen them relax and almost breathe a sigh of relief, even when they have been through a tough time, sometimes having lost a loved one of their own. I know it can be difficult to give them to a forever home, but I always know it is right for both of us, and when I hear back from the new family how well the new dog fits in, as if it has always been meant to be, it makes me get teary-eyed in a happy way. When people tell me they could never foster, as they would keep them all, or it is too sad, I challenge them with the fact that they are giving new hope to a dog, and it feels so good, it far outweighs any misgivings you may have had.
Rhonda with Earnhardt
Fostering is falling in love over and over again, and loving the dogs enough to let them go. It's seeing the love and joy when an adopter meets your foster for the first time and realizes they are meant for each other. It's occasional messed up floors, then pride in them as they learn to use the doggie door. It's when they eat your cell phone, and you know you can't be mad at that goofy sweet face. It's knowing you've given them a new life by taking them in and getting them ready for their new forever home. Why should you foster? No, it's not always easy, but the unconditional love of a dog who may or may not have experienced love before is worth it. You should foster because not only will it help the dog, but it will help you.
Susan with Roxie
I'm struck by the comment many people make, 'Oh, I could never foster.' I thought the same thing once.....but oh, the rewards! The most common question I get asked is, 'After spending so much time with these dogs, how do you give them up?' I always say, if I don't let them go, I can’t help any of the others.
Terry with Charlie Eskimo
I decided to foster the breed I know best, American Eskimo Dogs, Eskies, after watching too many returned to shelters by their prospective families. Eskies are really cute, all white and fluffy and alert. They do not do well being left along, they bark a lot, and they need mental and physical exercise. Of course I miss them and cry when they leave, but I also know they are with the right family because of the thorough application process. And if the adoption doesn't work out, they come back to me until the right home is found. I always have room in my heart and home for another Eskie in need of a forever home.

### POMDR Foster Needs

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/fosterneeds.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 2924

#### We Need Foster Homes!
We typically have about 80 dogs in our care at any given time. We also typically have up to 15 dogs waiting to get into foster homes.
POMDR provides supplies such as crate, bed, collar, harness, leash, id tag, bowls, toys, flea prevention. We also cover all medical expenses. We ask that our foster homes provide the food and a loving, safe home until your foster dog is adopted.
Fostering is a wonderful way to help dogs left behind find their forever homes. You may also want to foster as a way of finding the perfect match for your home if you are looking to add a dog to your family. [Click here](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/volunteer.html) for more information about volunteering and fostering and to fill out an online volunteer questionnaire.
Some dogs in our program are very popular, and multiple foster volunteers may be interested in fostering the same one. If the dog you are interested in is no longer available, please be patient, many others still need a foster home. Please note that some dogs may have medical or behavioral needs, which will be disclosed (if known) when you inquire about them. Thank you for your patience.
The following dogs need foster homes:
Miko
Looks like: Poodle
Male, 10 years old (estimated), 15 pounds
Miko is a happy, affectionate boy who enthusiastically greets everyone he meets with a joyful tail wag. He gets along well with other dogs and would love to find a family ready to enjoy plenty of walks, cuddles, and companionship. Miko would make a wonderful walking partner and a fantastic addition to just about any home!
If you're looking for a loving pup with a cheerful spirit and plenty of affection to share, Miko may be your perfect match. He's ready to bring happy tail wags, sweet moments, and lots of love to his forever family!
Miko joined POMDR from the SPCA of Monterey County when it was determined that his senior status made him a good fit for our program.
Miko is currently cozied up in a doggy suite, awaiting his foster family. If you are interested in giving this sweet senior a loving landing spot, please reach out today!
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Crystal
Looks like: Chihuahua, Long Coat
Female, 8 years old (estimated), 15 pounds
Crystal is a sweet, affectionate gal with a gentle and easygoing personality. She enjoys being around her people and is the kind of dog who will happily settle in beside you for companionship and cuddles. Crystal is friendly, loving, and ready to bring warmth and joy to her forever home.
Crystal gets along well with other dogs and would be happy to share her home with a canine companion. Her calm, pleasant nature makes her a wonderful little dog to have around, whether she's relaxing by your side or enjoying time with her furry friends.
Crystal joins Peace of Mind Dog Rescue from Hitchcock Road Animal Services after being found as a stray.
Crystal is currently cozied up in a doggy suite, awaiting her foster family. If you are interested in giving this sweet senior a loving landing spot, please reach out today!
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Bagel Boss
Looks like: English Bulldog
Male, 9 years old (estimated), 40 pounds
Bagel Boss is sweet, happy, and ready to snuggle up! He loves people and is good with dogs, though he is not very interested in other dogs. He has some pep and is playful, but knows how to settle, especially when his people aren't around--though he would prefer you to stay around.
Bagel Boss enjoys toys, and his ideal day would be playtime followed by naps and snuggles. Want a trusty companion? Bagel Boss is your pup!
Bagel Boss is joining POMDR after his guardian passed away.
Bagel Boss is a big boy and needs a foster before entering the POMDR program. Could that be you?
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Barnabas
Looks like: Poodle mix
Male, 15 years old (estimated), 28 pounds
Introducing Barnabas, the most charming "vampire" you will ever meet! With his soft, curly cream coat and gentle eyes, this sweet boy is far more interested in stealing your favorite spot on the couch than casting any spells. Barnabas is a gentle soul who loves human affection, making him the perfect companion to keep you warm and cozy all year round.
Barnabas joins us from San Jose Animal Care & Services. Due to his senior status, they felt that he would do best in a rescue setting to find his forever home.
Barnabas will be joining our program shortly and looking for a cozy landing spot. Can he stay with you? If you are interested in giving this sweet senior a loving landing spot, please reach out today!
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Evie
Looks like: Pitbull Terrier mix
Female, 9 years old (estimated), 85 pounds
Meet Evie, a whole lotta love wrapped up in one adorable potato of a Pittie! This sweet senior lady is as lovely as they come. Evie seems to think everyone she meets is a friend just waiting to happen, whether they have two legs or four! She's happy to make friends with dogs, kids, and even cats!
Evie is also a wonderful walking buddy! She's great on leash and would probably be thrilled to add a few more strolls to her daily routine. A little fresh air, some quality time with her people, and plenty of affection sound like Evie's idea of a pretty perfect life. And with a face that sweet and a body built for maximum snuggling, we suspect she'd be an excellent couch companion, too. If you're looking for a friendly, easygoing girl with a big heart and a little extra potato to love, Evie might just be your perfect match!
Evie joins Peace of Mind Dog Rescue after her guardian passed away.
Evie is looking for a loving landing spot to call her own. In order to join our program, Evie would need a foster home ready to welcome her. Interested in hosting this sweet girl? Please reach out today!
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Jason
Looks like: Maltese
Male, 11 years old (estimated), 8 pounds
Jason is a gentle soul who needs a little extra time and a whole lot of understanding to feel safe in the world. He's a timid boy who is still learning to trust, and he'll need a patient person who is happy to let him move at his own pace. Jason isn't ready to hit the walking trails just yet, and he may never be the adventurous, leash-walking type. But with time, kindness, and a little encouragement, he may surprise us! For now, his happy place is somewhere safe and cozy, with the freedom to take each new step when he's ready.
One of the best things about Jason is how wonderful he is with other dogs! In fact, having a confident canine companion (or even a whole pack!) in his next home is a must for this boy. He takes comfort in his fellow pups and can learn so much by watching them navigate the world. He also does well with kitties, making him a lovely candidate for a home with feline friends, too.
Jason is looking for someone who understands that progress doesn't always happen overnight. He may need extra time to settle in and build trust, but for the right person, helping him discover that the world can be a safe and happy place will be a very special journey.
Jason joins Peace of Mind Dog Rescue from Hitchcock Road Animal Services, where his senior status made him a good fit for our program.
Because Jason needs a delicate touch, he is looking for a special foster to build his confidence and trust. He's looking for a home with at least one other confident dog to learn from. Does this sound like you? Please reach out to us today.
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Boo
Looks like: Pit Bull, Husky
Female, 9 years old (estimated), 50 pounds
Boo! Did Boo surprise you? She didn't mean to! Boo is a sweet girl who likes other dogs and is great with people of all ages! She is pretty happy to relax on the couch, but likes a nice walk once or twice a day.
A proper lady, she's great on leash and knows her basic obedience. She is also crate trained! With her previous guardian, she would go to work, lighting up the day for everyone who saw her.
Despite her name, she's not scary at all! Boo is a big love looking for her next place to call home--just no cats, please. She promises no tricks, only treats! She is joining POMDR after her guardian could no longer care for her.
Boo is a big girl and needs a foster home before she can officially join the POMDR family. Can this girl curl up on your couch?
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Loretta
Looks like: Maltese mix
Female, 9 years old (estimated), 9 pounds
Loretta is ready to trade in her "rough around the edges" days for a life of cozy beds, gentle TLC, and being thoroughly spoiled. Loretta seems to have lived a tough life in the past and she's definitely in need of a little makeover and some extra love. She could use a nice bath, a good groom, a soft place to curl up, and most importantly, a person who will remind her just how wonderful she is.
Despite whatever she's been through, Loretta is an absolute sweetheart. She's easygoing, gentle, and gets along well with other dogs. She doesn't ask for much- just a comfy bed, some tasty snacks, a little companionship, and someone who will make sure she feels safe and loved from here on out.
Miss Loretta joins us from Hitchcock Road Animal Services after she was found as a stray. Due to her medical need and her senior status, they felt that she would do best in a rescue setting to find her forever home.
Loretta is currently cozied up in a doggy suite, awaiting her foster family. If you are interested in giving this sweet senior a loving landing spot, please reach out today!
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Gabby
Looks like: Schnauzer, Poodle mix
Female, 13 years old (estimated), 37 pounds
Gorgeous Gabby may be enjoying her golden years, but this sweet senior Schnoodle still has plenty of pep in her step! She loves her daily walks, basking in the sunshine, and staying close to her people for gentle pets and affection. With her moderate energy level, Gabby is the perfect companion for someone who enjoys a leisurely stroll followed by a cozy cuddle session on the couch.
Gabby is as easygoing as they come. She gets along wonderfully with both dogs and cats, making her a great fit for many types of homes. She isn't demanding or overbearing - just a calm, loving presence with soulful eyes that have a way of capturing everyone's heart. Gabby is also delightfully quiet, usually only offering a bark or two every few days when she's hoping for a little extra attention.
One of Gabby's many wonderful qualities is that she is fully housetrained, according to her foster. In fact, she arrived without even a single accident, which is a rare and impressive testament to what a good girl she is.
Since arriving, Gabby has been receiving the medical care she needs to help her feel her best. She is currently being treated for Cushing's disease and is working toward losing approximately 9 pounds. Her weight loss journey will take time, even with reduced calories, and may require several months of patience and consistency. Reaching a healthier weight is an important step before she can safely undergo a dental cleaning and have a couple of lipomas removed.
Gabby is also an enthusiastic eater and has a habit of consuming things she shouldn't. Her future family will need to provide a safe environment by keeping potentially harmful items out of reach, including products such as snail or rat bait.
Gabby is a special senior girl who deserves a home where her health needs are understood and where she can spend her days feeling safe, loved, and cherished. If you believe life's sweetest companions only get better with age, Gabby is ready to show you just how wonderful a senior dog can be.
Gabby joined the POMDR pack after her loving guardian was no longer able to care for her.
Gabby needs to move fosters soon! Interested in meeting her? Please reach out so that you can come meet this precious girl and let her fill your home and heart with love.
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Barbara Walters
Looks like: French Bulldog
Female, 7 years old (estimated), 20 pounds
Barbara Walters is just as charismatic and personable as her namesake! She's the perfect size to sit by your side and bend an ear (or both ears!) and she's still got plenty of spunk and moxie!
She enjoys interviewing adults, children, and other dogs, though she's yet to meet a cat. She is also crate-trained and knows her basic commands. Barbara is still peppy and ready to get back into interview shape! Lights! Camera! Foster!
Barbara Walters joined POMDR after her guardian was no longer able to care for her.
Barbara is looking to join POMDR on 9/2 and is hoping to go straight to a foster! Can you help her get back on-screen?
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.
Rita
Looks like: Maltese
Female, 13 years old (estimated), 11 pounds
Rita is a gal with a heart full of love and a calm, easygoing charm. She's the kind of companion who's happiest in the simple moments: cozy snuggles, gentle strolls, and quietly keeping you company just because she loves being near you. Rita is always ready to settle in close at your feet, beside you on the couch, or wherever her favorite person happens to be. She doesn't ask for much, just your presence, patience, and a soft spot to land. She's cat friendly and has done well living with feline housemates.
Rita is gentle and sensitive and prefers not to be picked up or moved abruptly, and with consistency and care, she settles in beautifully. Her twice-daily medication is part of her routine and she does well when it's thoughtfully tucked into meals.
Rita came to POMDR after her guardian moved into assisted living and could no longer care for her. Now she's ready for her next chapter,a quiet, loving home where she can share her steady devotion, soft companionship, and endless sweetness.
Darling Rita has been showing a little bit of cognitive decline in her aging years. While she has shared a home with another dog much of her life, she seems to wish to be in a home free from other dogs. We'd love for Rita to cozy up in a loving retirement spot.
If you are interested in fostering and you are already a POMDR volunteer, email us. If you are interested in fostering and are not a volunteer yet, please fill out a volunteer application. Or, if you're interested in adopting, fill out an adoption questionnaire.

#### Benefit Shop - Where Hidden Gems are Found
[image: Benefit Shop Montage]
223 Grand Ave, Suite 1
Pacific Grove
(831) 312-8991
Tuesday - Saturday 11:00 am - 5:00 pm
(closed Sunday & Monday)
The POMDR Benefit Shop specializes in higher-end lightly used merchandise at reasonable prices.
If you are interested in volunteering at the Benefit Shop, please [fill out an application](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRVolunteerApplication.php).
Please come check out our art work, clothing, jewelry, home goods, furniture and other merchandise.
If you’d like to make a donation, contact [shop@pomdr.org](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#7c0f14130c3c0c1311180e52130e1b). Please let us know what you have to donate and your preferred day and time frame. Thank you!
#### Items We Take
- Accessories such as jewelry, purses and scarves
- Home decor such as artwork and frames
- Lamps and lighting
- Diningware such as dish sets and glassware
- Patio decor
- Seasonal and holiday decor items (in season)
- Vintage and collectibles for the home
- Table Linens
- Dressers, book cases, coffee and end tables, chairs and kitchen/dining room tables. (If your item is LARGE, please email a photo to shop@pomdr.org and our shop manager will get back to you to ensure we have space for your item.)
- Books
- Gardening items
- Sports equipment – sports balls, hand weights, small equipment, etc.
- Toys and games
- Clothing - men's and women's lightly worn items
#### Items We Cannot Use
- Mattresses
- Baby equipment such as car seats and high chairs due to safety regulations and recall
- Upholstered furniture of any type
- Hangers
- Medical and mobility equipment such as a walkers and portable toilet seats
- TVs
- Toxic materials and already opened paint cans.
- Anything in disrepair, dirty, moldy or mildewed

### POMDR Help Placing Your Dog

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/helpplacingdog.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 400

#### Help Placing Your Dog - Guardian Surrender Program
Si necesita ayuda para colocar a su perro y necesita ayuda para completar un cuestionario de admisión en línea, puede llamarnos al (831) 718-9122. Tenga en cuenta que esto retrasará el procesamiento de su solicitud.
Peace of Mind Dog Rescue focuses on taking in senior dogs from shelters and taking in dogs from senior guardians who can no longer care for them. There are times in our lives when we have to make self-care a priority. And unfortunately there are circumstances, like declining health, that may require finding a new home for your dog. It can be one of the most difficult but also most loving and selfless decisions you may need to make. POMDR is here to help. We focus on helping dogs and people from Monterey, Santa Cruz, and San Benito counties.
We also take in dogs by prearrangement through our [Perpetual Care Program](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/lifetimecare.html).
If you need to surrender a dog, please fill out an [online intake questionnaire](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRIntakeQuestionnaire.php). (If you need assistance filling out the questionnaire, you can call us at (831) 718-9122. Please be aware that this will delay the processing of your request.)
Before POMDR can intake your dog, we will need to meet the dog. Dogs who come to POMDR must be good with other dogs (no aggression) and must be good with people (no bite history or propensity to bite).
We are equipped to handle a variety of medical cases and senior-related issues, but some extreme medical cases might be difficult for us to take on, e.g. Epilepsy, Diabetes, Paralysis, Megaesophagus, Advanced Dementia. Medical intakes are evaluated on a case by case basis.
If you don't think your dog is a POMDR candidate, you can still fill out an intake questionnaire and we can refer you to additional resources.
If your dog is accepted into our program, we will provide the following:
- Placement in a foster home until she is adopted.
- High quality dog food.
- Coverage of any medical needs that come up while she is in our care.
- Socialization and companionship.
- A safety net for the lifetime of your dog should she ever need to find a new home at any point in her life.
- Grooming and flea control while she is in our care.
- Screening of potential adopters including home visit and reference check.
- Annual follow-ups and home visits to ensure the placement has been successful.
Any donation made will also help us rescue more dogs left behind.

### POMDR Lifetime Care

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/lifetimecare.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 673

#### Lifetime Care for Your Dog - Perpetual Care Program
What would happen to your dog if something happens to you?
Take a moment to think about what would happen to your dog if something happened to you. Do you have written instructions with the name and contact info of the person that will care for your dog? Has the new guardian agreed in no uncertain terms to make your dog a part of their family if you can no longer care for him/her?
None of us likes to imagine what our life would be like without our dog, or worse, what our dog’s life would be like without us. It is important to pre-arrange for the future care of your dog in case you are no longer able to care for him due to accident, illness, or death. Once these arrangements are made, you can have peace of mind that your dog will not end up alone and frightened in an animal shelter. The best way to know that your dog will be cared for in the event of your death is to consult with your attorney to name a guardian for your dog and create a pet trust.
In the event that you do not have a friend or family member who is able to care for your dog, Peace of Mind’s Perpetual Care Program ensures that dog guardians, who plan in advance for the care of their dog, will be guaranteed a loving, warm, and safe home for their dog(s). Once your dog is in our care, he will be cared for in a foster home either for the rest of his life or until a perfect match with a new permanent guardian is found.
[Listen to an NPR story](http://kazu.org/post/what-happens-if-we-outlive-our-pets-pg-non-profit-has-answer) about our Perpetual Care Program
Perpetual Care Program: How It Works
To enroll your beloved dog in our Perpetual Care Program, we suggest that you set up a legal pet trust for the lifetime care expenses for your dog in the event of your illness, injury, or death. We also suggest that you make an annual gift in any amount to POMDR to support our mission to be here for dogs left behind. If you are unable to set up a pet trust or make an annual gift, but would still like to enroll your dog in our Perpetual Care Program, please contact us to discuss the next steps.
Your dog's pet trust will ensure the resources are available for the lifetime care of your dog whether in a POMDR foster home or a new loving permanent home. We will need to meet your dog to determine if POMDR can accept him/her into our program. The eligibility for enrollment will be determined by our staff and will depend upon the dog’s age, size, temperament, and any special medical needs.
Dogs enrolled in POMDR's Perpetual Care Program will receive the following:
- Placement in a loving foster home at any point that you are no longer able to care for your dog.
- A lifetime commitment from POMDR. We will ensure that your dog will be cared for by a POMDR foster home or a new adoptive home for the rest of their life.
By setting up a pet trust for your dog, you are guaranteed loving care for your dog when you are no longer able to provide care yourself. At the time of your dog's death, any remainder of the trust would go into POMDR's general fund to help more dogs. Your gift will carry on the legacy of your love and respect for dogs by enabling POMDR to continue to rescue dogs in need.
Please call us at (831) 718-9122 or email us for more information about our Perpetual Care Program or to speak with a staff member about enrollment.
Please speak with your attorney or visit our [resources page](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/resources.html) for more information on setting up a pet trust and planning for your pet's future should you predecease them.
For more information, visit our [page of Frequently Asked Questions](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/lifetimecareFAQ.html).

#### POMDR Lifetime Care

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/lifetimecareFAQ.html  
Linked from: POMDR Lifetime Care  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 612

#### Frequently Asked Questions about our Perpetual Care Program
- How do I enroll my dog in the Perpetual Care Program?
  Contact POMDR and talk with a board member to determine if your dog is appropriate for our program. Once it is determined that your dog can enroll in our program, we will give you a Pet Profile form to fill out so we know your dog’s likes, dislikes, habits, routines, health history, what vet clinic has his records, and anything else you would like us to know about your dog. You will then need to set up a pet trust with an attorney to provide the funds to care for your dog for the rest of his life.
- What if my dog is old or in poor health?
  If you enroll your dog in the Perpetual Care Program, POMDR will accept your dog regardless of his or her physical condition. Although older and ailing animals are sometimes difficult to place in permanent homes, POMDR will provide foster care for your dog as long as needed. You will want to take your dog’s age and health into account when funding his pet trust.
- What services will the Perpetual Care Program provide for my dog if I die or can no longer care for him?
  POMDR will become your dog’s legal guardian, and will start caring for your pet as soon as we receive notice of your death or incapacitation. We will then place your pet up for adoption to a suitable, loving home consistent with the information in his or her Pet Profile. Enrollment in our Perpetual Care Program ensures that your dog will receive compassionate care for the rest of his or her life.
- Do I need to pay anything up front?
  No. The Perpetual Care Program is funded through a pet trust that you set up with your attorney. There is no membership fee or cost to you during your lifetime, but a donation at the time of setting up your trust or leaving a donation to POMDR in your estate planning will help support POMDR’s mission of caring for dogs left behind.
- I don’t have much money, but I have a life insurance policy and some physical assets. Can I still participate in the Perpetual Care Program?
  You will want to talk with your attorney about funding your dog’s Pet Trust with non-liquid assets to determine if this option will work for you and your dog.
- Will my pet be adopted through the Perpetual Care Program?
  POMDR will make every effort to find your dog a new, permanent home. We will post his or her photograph and life story on our website, and bring him or her to our adoption events. Although we cannot absolutely guarantee that your dog will be adopted, the great majority of our dogs find new homes with our help. We will have a lifetime commitment to your dog whether he is in a foster home or a permanent home.
- Where will my dog live while he or she is awaiting a new home?
  While we work to find a permanent home for your dog, he or she will live in a qualified foster home with a POMDR volunteer. Our foster volunteers are sensitive to the special needs of surviving pets, and will provide love and attention as well as physical care. In the event that we do not have a foster home available for your dog, we will board him or her in a comfortable boarding facility with regular walks and outings provided by our volunteers until he or she is adopted or placed in a foster home.

### POMDR Helping Paw

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/helpingpaw.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 491

#### Helping Paw Program
Si no puede completar una solicitud o necesita más información, puede llamarnos al (831) 718-9122. Tenga en cuenta que esto retrasará el procesamiento de su solicitud.
Our Helping Paw program assists seniors, hospice patients and other pet guardians facing challenging times in keeping their beloved dogs and cats, providing a bridge that allows them stay together as long as possible.
When people are physically or financially unable to care for their pets, Peace of Mind Dog Rescue would like to lend a helping paw.
Our Helping Paw program provides both volunteer assistance and financial assistance.
#### Helping Paw Financial Assistance
Geography: Monterey, Santa Cruz and San Benito Counties.
Income: You must be able to demonstrate financial need in order to qualify for financial assistance.
We cover: Urgent medical needs that will alleviate an animal's suffering. We cover expenses for dogs and cats.
We do not cover: Routine veterinary care. (We can provide flea treatment when available by pre-arrangement. You must be able to pick it up at our office in Pacific Grove.)
Maximum assistance: $750. We can help with up to $2,750 for major medical emergencies.
Exam and estimate: It is best if your pet has had an exam and you can provide us with an estimate for care needed. In some circumstances, we can cover the cost of the exam.
Annual grants: Our financial assistance is meant to be a safety net during difficult times with a limit of assistance once per year.
Spay/Neuter Requirement: All pets must be spayed or neutered to receive funding. If they are not already spayed or neutered, POMDR will cover the cost to spay/neuter at the time of treatment or shortly after.
Apply for Financial Assistance
[Solicitar Ayuda Financiera](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRHelpingPawFinanceApplicationSpanish.php)
#### Helping Paw Volunteer Assistance
We provide: Short term dog walking for emergency situations, emergency short term foster care or boarding for people who are in the hospital or recovering from illness or surgery, transportation to vet or groomer for disabled and senior pet guardians.
Requirements: To ensure the safety of our volunteers and the success of foster placements and walking brigades, all dogs must pass a behavioral evaluation prior to receiving dog walking or foster care services. This evaluation typically takes place at our office in Pacific Grove.
Geography: Monterey, Santa Cruz, San Benito County residents only. Subject to volunteer availability in the area.
Income: There are no income restrictions for Volunteer Assistance. Donations are welcomed.
[Apply for Volunteer Assistance](https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/POMDRHelpingPawVolunteerApplication.php)
#### Helping Paw Supplies
We can provide the following supplies that have been donated to us, as available. They must be picked up at our headquarters in Pacific Grove.
- Dog and Cat Food
- Flea prevention
- Collars
- Leashes
- Crates
- ID tags
- Blankets
- Dog jackets, sweaters, raincoats
#### Need Further Assistance?
If you are unable to fill out an application or need more information, you can call us at (831) 718-9122. Please be aware that this will delay the processing of your request.
Just one of the many households we’ve been able to help through our Helping Paw program.

### POMDR Resources

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/resources.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.

#### Resources
We have compiled some resources for senior dogs and senior people. We hope you find this information helpful.
#### Books
[When Your Pet Outlives You: Protecting Animal Companions After You Die](https://www.amazon.com/When-Your-Pet-Outlives-You/dp/0939165449)
Donald Congalton & Charlotte Alexander
When Your Pet Outlives You is an easy-to-use resource guide designed to help pet owners deal with one of their major, overriding fears: If they die tomorrow, what will happen to their beloved companion animals? This is the first book to address this issue at length, combining personal stories with step-by-step information on how to prepare today, along with examples of legal documents, resources, and an index.
[All My Children Wear Fur Coats: How to Leave a Legacy for Your Dog](http://www.legacyforyourpet.com/)
Peggy R. Hoyt
All My Children Wear Fur Coats is about leaving a legacy for your pet and is a pet estate planning book. The Hall Mark of True Love is planning for your pet's future without you and for your future without your pet.
[Perpetual Care: Who Will Look After Your Dogs if You’re Not Around?](https://www.amazon.com/PerPETual-Care-after-Youre-Around/dp/0965250288)
Lisa Rogak
In this groundbreaking book, you’ll learn the pros and cons of setting up a pet trust, how to select a permanent caretaker, and how to come up with a plan that makes everybody—including your pets—happy.
#### Check-in Services
[Iamfine](http://info.iamfine.com)
Iamfine is a daily check-in service to confirm that senior citizens living alone are OK. If you live alone and have pets, this service could save their life or yours. You set a time to be called each day. Iamfine calls you and you press “1” on your phone. You are now checked in for the day.
[Snug](https://www.snugsafe.com/)
Snug is a free app that checks in daily to make sure you’re okay - and will let someone know if you are not.
#### Financial Assistance for Pet Guardians
[Assistance Dog Special Allowance Program](https://www.cdss.ca.gov/assistance-dogs)
Provides a monthly payment of $50 to eligible persons who use a guide, signal, or service dog to help them with their disability-related needs.
[BirchBark Foundation](https://www.birchbarkfoundation.org)
Provides financial assistance to pet guardians needing assistance with veterinary expenses. Serving Monterey and Santa Cruz Counties.
[BluePearl Cares](https://bluepearlvet.com/bluepearl-cares/)
Exclusive to BluePearl clients. Surgery must be done through BluePearl.
[Brown Dog Foundation](https://www.browndogfoundation.org/ask-for-help)
ExclusiveYou must apply online and a pre-screening application. if you qualify, a case manager will reach out and start the process. They do not work with Blue Pearl Vet Services anymore.
[CareCredit](https://www.carecredit.com)
The CareCredit credit card is a simple, flexible way to pay for veterinary care for your pets. See if you prequalify without impacting your credit score.
[Free Animal Doctor](https://freeanimaldoctor.org/)
Crowdfund individual cases for pets needing expensive procedures, pay directly to veterinarian.
[Magic Bullet Cure](https://themagicbulletfund.org/apply/)
Only for pets who are diagnosed with cancer who will be having surgery and need chemotherapy (funds can only be used for surgery or chemo). This org does not give grants but with guardian participation, they help crowdfund.
[The Mosby Foundation](https://themosbyfoundation.org/apply-for-aid/)
Provides financial assistance for critically sick and injured dogs.
[Paws 4 A Cure](https://www.paws4acure.org/askforhelp.php)
Only help with medications, insulin, heart worm treatment and medical equipment (like doggy wheelchairs). Maximum amount of assistance is $500 and it is a one-time grant.
[The Pet Fund](https://www.thepetfund.com/about-us)
A national nonprofit dedicated to funding veterinary care for those who could not afford it.
[Pets Find Help](https://pets.findhelp.com/)
Search engine for resources using zip code (includes food pantry, financial assistance, pet friendly housing, respite care, etc).
[Pets of the Homeless](https://petsofthehomeless.org/)
Financial assistance for vet care and pet food to unhoused individuals. Must call and do application over the phone.
[SAGE Compassion for Animals](https://www.sagec4a.org/apply/)
Provides financial assistance to pet guardians seeking veterinary care for immediate life-threatening injuries or illnesses. They do not cover initial exam fees or diagnostics. Serving Alameda, Contra Costa, Marin, Napa, San Francisco, San Mateo, Santa Clara, Solano and Sonoma counties in California.
[Santa Cruz SPCA](https://spcasc.org/dr-jeans-senior-friends/)
Financial assistance (grants up to $500, no dentals) for low-income seniors with dogs over six years old living in Santa Cruz County. Also sponsors two annual wellness days for free preventative care.
[Saving Gracie](https://www.saving-gracie.org/angel-fund-apply-for-help/)
Diagnostics must be complete, estimate must be ready, pet must have a good prognosis with no pre-existing conditions, must have exhausted every resource including savings and credit cards.
[Second Chances for Blind Dogs](https://secondchancesforblinddogs.org/application-for-cocos-eye-care-grant)
Provides financial assistance for low-income guardians who have dogs who are blind, have impaired sight or are needing eye care including enucleations, cherry eye procedures and eye surgeries.
[Waggle](https://www.waggle.org/)
Crowdfund up to $2,000 per case, pay directly to veterinarian.
#### Grief Support
National Pet Loss Grief Support Hotline
Toll free. (866) 266-8635. Monday-Thursday 7-9pm Pacific Time. Saturday 1-3pm Pacific Time. Messages can be left at other times and will be returned.
[BirchBark Pet Loss and Grief Support Group](http://www.birchbarkfoundation.org/pet-loss-grief-support/)
Pet loss can result in overwhelming feelings of grief, sadness, and loss. The BirchBark Foundation of Santa Cruz offers a safe and comforting place for pet families who are grieving the loss of their pet.
#### Life-enhancing Products for Senior Animals and Senior People
[KittyKaddy](http://www.kittykaddy.com/)
KittyKaddy™ pet bowls are designed to meet the demands of pet owners with special needs. If you have difficulty bending or stooping down to pick up your dog's or cat's bowl, the KittyKaddy and K-9 Kaddy double diners can enhance your ability to care for your pet.
[Lucky and Loyal](https://luckyandloyal.com/)
Custom orthopedic support for dogs. A unique two piece set that when used together will support (vest) and exercise (sleeves) the entire dog! Your dog will maintain muscle strength and will regain natural quadruped motion. Some special cases where the dog is truly struggling to get up require "severe weakness" sleeves. These anchor in different places. Also, some dogs have a very wide chest and need a wider vest and neck extenders are also available.
[Ruffwear Harness](http://www.ruffwear.com/Web-Master-Harness?sc=2&category=1131)
Originally designed for search-and-rescue organizations, the Web Master lets you assist your dog up and down in a variety of situations, and can also be used during rehabilitation. A generous ergonomic cut allows for a full range of motion, while still providing security.
[Skid Safe](http://www.ndclean.com/skid-safe-water-based-sealer--finish)
Use SKID SAFE to make your slippery floors highly slip resistant. Now you can dramatically reduce slip and fall hazards on slick/slippery floors, and the maintenance costs at the same time.
[Toe Grips](https://www.toegrips.com)
ToeGrips are nonslip nail grips that provide instant traction through dogs’ toenails. Designed for senior and special needs dogs, ToeGrips stop dog slipping on floors and stairs, including hardwood, laminate, and tile.
#### Pet Trusts
[Clark Ruggiero Law Office](https://clarkruggiero.com/)
Will create a free pet trust for people leaving a dog to Peace of Mind Dog Rescue.
[ASPCA - Pet Trusts](https://www.aspca.org/pet-care/pet-planning/frequently-asked-questions)
Frequently asked questions about pet trusts.
[GoodRX](https://www.goodrx.com/pet-health/pets/pet-in-will-trust?&utm_source=sfmc&utm_medium=email&utm_campaign=3670&utm_term=Topic2_Text&utm_id=9969&sfmc_id=314070&utm_content=weekly&utm_param2=04-04-24)
How to provide for a pet in a will or trust.
[Professor Beyer - Estate Planning for Pets](http://www.professorbeyer.com/Articles/Animals.html)
Frequently asked questions about pet trusts.
[2nd Chance for Pets](http://www.2ndchance4pets.org/)
A nationwide organization which provides pet guardians, veterinarians, and members of animal care organizations with comprehensive information and lifetime care solutions to ensure care for companion animals. They provide access to valuable information that will assist you with arranging lifetime care for pets.
#### Senior Cat Rescues
[Golden Oldies Cat Rescue](http://www.gocatrescue.org)
Golden Oldies Cat Rescue is a resource and advocate for older cats in Monterey County. They re-home cats who are at risk of euthanasia in shelters or whose guardians can no longer care for them. They place these cats, who have no other options of a safe place to go, into temporary foster care. Then find them permanent loving homes, enriching the lives of both the cats and their new families.
They promise a lifetime commitment to the cats in their care. They either will be adopted or will live out their lives in a foster homes.
Golden Oldies is led by a board who share a vision of helping older cats in Monterey County live out their remaining years in safe, secure and loving homes. They are all volunteer and 100 percent foster-based.
#### Senior Dog Rescues
[Muttville](http://www.muttville.org/)
Muttville is a non-profit, 501(c)3 organization, based in San Francisco, CA dedicated to improving the lives of senior dogs. On a local level, Muttville rescues senior dogs and finds them new homes or gives them hospice. On a global level, Muttville provides information about caring for older dogs and support for people who do.
[Old Dog Haven](http://www.olddoghaven.org/)
OldDog Haven is a small nonprofit group, based in the state of Washington, using a large network of people to provide a loving safe home for abandoned senior dogs. Our goal is to provide a loving, safe home for senior dogs abandoned at this stage of their lives. When we have room, and the means, we take these dogs into our homes; if possible we adopt out those with a reasonable life-expectancy. We care for the rest as long as they have good quality of life. In addition, we try to assist owners in finding new homes for their senior dogs through our website and referrals.
[Senior Dog Project](http://www.srdogs.com/)
The Senior Dogs Project site presents a broad listing of various agencies that help to rehome senior dogs (over the age of five). You can find a dog to adopt or seek help in placing a dog through these agencies.
[Tails of Gray](http://www.tailsofgray.org)
Tails of Gray is dedicated to saving the lives of innocent senior dogs left homeless for any reason, providing quality medical care and placing them into loving, responsible and permanent homes whenever possible.

### POMDR Thank You!

Address: https://www.peaceofminddogrescue.org/thankyou.html  
Meta description: Peace of Mind Dog Rescue is dedicated to finding new loving homes for dogs whose guardians can no longer care for them due to illness, death, or other challenging circumstances, and to finding homes for senior dogs in animal shelters.  
Words: 1161

#### Thank You!
We would like to thank the individuals, businesses, and foundations that make it possible for us to save the lives of dogs left behind. We couldn't do it without you!
#### Keystone Donors (over $100,000 donated)
#### Over $1,000,000 donated
Patricia J. Bauer for donating funds to purchase and create the POMDR Patricia J. Bauer Center at 615 Forest Avenue in Pacific Grove and for providing funding to help with medical expenses for our dogs.
Animal Welfare and Assistance Group for donating to expand our Helping Paw Program.
#### Over $500,000 donated
Anonymous in Honor of Gina Canine and All the Orphans for a donation to help pay, attract, hire, and retain veterinary and support staff at the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic as well as to provide medical care for the dogs we rescue.
The Boand Family Foundation for donating to our general fund and for being our lead donor to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic and the Boand House.
George and Debra Couch for donating to our general fund, for sponsoring our Lucky Dog Gala and for donating funds to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Barbara De Groodt for directing a donation to POMDR from the Estate of Dale Nakashima.
#### Over $100,000 donated
Anonymous in Honor of Carie Broecker and Monica Rua for donating to our Helping Paw Program and our general fund.
Betty Bass for leaving a generous donation to POMDR in her estate plan.
Susan Branson for leaving a generous donation to POMDR in her estate plan.
Eric and Ila Davis Fund for donating to our Helping Paw Program and our general fund.
Shera Falk for donating to our general fund.
Friends of Animals Foundation for donating to our general fund and to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Mary Jo and Hank Greenberg Animal Welfare Foundation for donating for medical care for the dogs we rescue.
Harden Foundation for donating to help with medical expenses and to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Michele Ikuta-Alvarado for leaving a generous donation to POMDR in her estate plan.
Debbie Landi for donating to cover program salaries for several years and for donating to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Lucinda McDermott, PhD for donating to our general fund.
Monterey Peninsula Foundation for veterinary equipment and general operating expenses.
F. Robert Nunes for donating to our general fund and to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Perrine Charitable Foundation for providing the seed money to start POMDR.
Donna Ritter for leaving a generous donation to POMDR in her estate plan.
Dawn Runyon for leaving a generous donation to POMDR in her estate plan.
Barbara Swain for donating to our general fund and to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Tom and Jennifer Werbe for donating to cover program salaries for 2 years, and for sponsoring our Lucky Dog Gala for four years, and for donating to create the POMDR Boand Veterinary Clinic.
Zoe’s Cruises and Tours for donating to our medical fund.
